</div><div class="mw-content-ltr mw-parser-output" lang="en" dir="ltr"><div class="shortdescription nomobile noexcerpt noprint searchaux" style="display:none">System or group governing an organized community</div>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1320445320">.mw-parser-output .hatnote{font-style:italic}.mw-parser-output div.hatnote{padding-left:1.6em;margin-bottom:0.5em}.mw-parser-output .hatnote i{font-style:normal}.mw-parser-output .hatnote+link+.hatnote{margin-top:-0.5em}@media print{body.ns-0 .mw-parser-output .hatnote{display:none!important}}</style><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">"Gov" redirects here. For other uses, see <a href="/wiki/Gov_(disambiguation)" class="mw-disambig" title="Gov (disambiguation)">Gov (disambiguation)</a>.</div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1320445320" /><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">For the executive power referred to as "the government", see <a href="/wiki/Executive_(government)" title="Executive (government)">Executive (government)</a>. For other uses, see <a href="/wiki/Government_(disambiguation)" class="mw-disambig" title="Government (disambiguation)">Government (disambiguation)</a>.</div>

<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Forms_of_government.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/04/Forms_of_government.svg/500px-Forms_of_government.svg.png" decoding="async" width="430" height="218" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/04/Forms_of_government.svg/960px-Forms_of_government.svg.png 1.5x" data-file-width="2754" data-file-height="1398" /></a><figcaption><div class="collapsible-list mw-collapsible" style="text-align: left;"> <div style="line-height: 1.6em; font-weight: bold;"><div>World's states colored by systems of <a class="mw-selflink selflink">government</a>:</div></div> <ul class="mw-collapsible-content" style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; line-height: inherit; list-style: none; margin-left: 0;"><li style="line-height: inherit; margin: 0"> <hr /> <b><a href="/wiki/Parliamentary_system" title="Parliamentary system">Parliamentary systems</a>:</b> Head of government is elected or nominated by and accountable to the legislature. <style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r981673959">.mw-parser-output .legend{page-break-inside:avoid;break-inside:avoid-column}.mw-parser-output .legend-color{display:inline-block;min-width:1.25em;height:1.25em;line-height:1.25;margin:1px 0;text-align:center;border:1px solid black;background-color:transparent;color:black}.mw-parser-output .legend-text{}</style><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:#db060b; color:white;-webkit-print-color-adjust: exact; print-color-adjust: exact;">&#160;</span>&#160;<a href="/wiki/Constitutional_monarchy" title="Constitutional monarchy">Constitutional monarchy</a> with a ceremonial monarch</div> <link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959" /><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:#f4a637; color:black;-webkit-print-color-adjust: exact; print-color-adjust: exact;">&#160;</span>&#160;<a href="/wiki/Parliamentary_republic" title="Parliamentary republic">Parliamentary republic</a> with a ceremonial president</div> <link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959" /><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:#4db264; color:black;-webkit-print-color-adjust: exact; print-color-adjust: exact;">&#160;</span>&#160;<a href="/wiki/Parliamentary_republics_with_an_executive_presidency" title="Parliamentary republics with an executive presidency">Parliamentary republic with an executive president</a></div> <hr /> <b><a href="/wiki/Presidential_system" title="Presidential system">Presidential system</a>:</b> Head of government (president) is popularly elected and independent of the legislature. <link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959" /><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:#1965b0; color:white;-webkit-print-color-adjust: exact; print-color-adjust: exact;">&#160;</span>&#160;Presidential republic</div> <hr /> <b>Hybrid systems:</b> <link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959" /><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:#f7f057; color:black;-webkit-print-color-adjust: exact; print-color-adjust: exact;">&#160;</span>&#160;<a href="/wiki/Semi-presidential_republic" class="mw-redirect" title="Semi-presidential republic">Semi-presidential republic</a>: Executive president is independent of the legislature; head of government is appointed by the president and is accountable to the legislature.</div> <link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959" /><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:#7bb0df; color:black;-webkit-print-color-adjust: exact; print-color-adjust: exact;">&#160;</span>&#160;<a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_by_system_of_government#Assembly-independent_republics" title="List of countries by system of government">Assembly-independent republic</a>: Head of government (president or directory) is elected by the legislature, but is not accountable to it.</div> <hr /> <b>Other systems:</b> <link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959" /><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:#90c886; color:black;-webkit-print-color-adjust: exact; print-color-adjust: exact;">&#160;</span>&#160;<a href="/wiki/Theocracy" title="Theocracy">Theocratic</a> republic: Supreme leader is head of both the state and the faith, and holds significant executive and legislative power</div> <link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959" /><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:#ae76a3; color:black;-webkit-print-color-adjust: exact; print-color-adjust: exact;">&#160;</span>&#160;<a href="/wiki/Constitutional_monarchy#Executive_monarchy_versus_ceremonial_monarchy" title="Constitutional monarchy">Semi-constitutional monarchy</a>: Monarch holds significant executive or legislative power but is still restricted by the constitution.</div> <link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959" /><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:#882e72; color:white;-webkit-print-color-adjust: exact; print-color-adjust: exact;">&#160;</span>&#160;<a href="/wiki/Absolute_monarchy" title="Absolute monarchy">Absolute monarchy</a>: Monarch has unlimited power.</div> <link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959" /><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:#71190e; color:white;-webkit-print-color-adjust: exact; print-color-adjust: exact;">&#160;</span>&#160;<a href="/wiki/One-party_state" title="One-party state">One-party state</a>: Power is constitutionally linked to a single political party.</div> <link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959" /><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:#cae1ac; color:black;-webkit-print-color-adjust: exact; print-color-adjust: exact;">&#160;</span>&#160;<a href="/wiki/Military_junta" title="Military junta">Military junta</a>: Committee of military leaders controls the government; constitutional provisions are suspended.</div> <link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959" /><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:#777777; color:black;-webkit-print-color-adjust: exact; print-color-adjust: exact;">&#160;</span>&#160;<a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_by_system_of_government#Governments_without_a_permanent_constitution" title="List of countries by system of government">Governments with no constitutional basis</a>: No constitutionally defined basis to current regime, i.e., <a href="/wiki/Provisional_government" title="Provisional government">provisional governments</a> or <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_by_system_of_government#Islamic_theocracy" title="List of countries by system of government">Islamic theocracies</a>.</div> <link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959" /><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:#C0C0C0; color:black;-webkit-print-color-adjust: exact; print-color-adjust: exact;">&#160;</span>&#160;Dependent territories or places without governments</div> <hr /> <b>Note:</b> this chart represents the <span title="Latin-language text"><i lang="la"><a href="/wiki/De_jure" title="De jure">de jure</a></i></span> systems of government, not the <span title="Latin-language text"><i lang="la"><a href="/wiki/De_facto" title="De facto">de facto</a></i></span> degree of democracy. <style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1333133064">.mw-parser-output .hlist dl,.mw-parser-output .hlist <div class="sidebar-list mw-collapsible mw-collapsed"><div class="sidebar-list-title" style="background:#ddddff;;color: var(--color-base)"><div class="sidebar-list-title-c">Related topics</div></div><div class="sidebar-list-content mw-collapsible-content">

</tr><tr><td class="sidebar-navbar"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1333133064" /><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1239400231" /><div class="navbar plainlinks hlist navbar-mini"><ul><li class="nv-view"><a href="/wiki/Template:Governance" title="Template:Governance"><abbr title="View this template">v</abbr></a></li><li class="nv-talk"><a href="/wiki/Template_talk:Governance" title="Template talk:Governance"><abbr title="Discuss this template">t</abbr></a></li><li class="nv-edit"><a href="/wiki/Special:EditPage/Template:Governance" title="Special:EditPage/Template:Governance"><abbr title="Edit this template">e</abbr></a></li></ul></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>A <b>government</b> is the system or group of people governing an organized community, generally a <a href="/wiki/State_(polity)" title="State (polity)">state</a>.
</p><p>In the case of its broad associative definition, government normally consists of <a href="/wiki/Legislature" title="Legislature">legislature</a>, <a href="/wiki/Executive_(government)" title="Executive (government)">executive</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Judiciary" title="Judiciary">judiciary</a>. Government is a means by which organizational <a href="/wiki/Policies" class="mw-redirect" title="Policies">policies</a> are enforced, as well as a mechanism for determining policy. In many countries, the government has a kind of <a href="/wiki/Constitution" title="Constitution">constitution</a>, a statement of its governing principles and philosophy.
</p><p>While all types of organizations have <a href="/wiki/Governance" title="Governance">governance</a>, the term <i>government</i> is often used more specifically to refer to the approximately 200 <a href="/wiki/List_of_sovereign_states" title="List of sovereign states">independent national governments</a> and <a href="/wiki/Government_agency" title="Government agency">subsidiary organizations</a>.
</p><p>The main types of modern <a href="/wiki/Political_system" title="Political system">political systems</a> recognized are <a href="/wiki/Democracy" title="Democracy">democracies</a>, <a href="/wiki/Totalitarian_regime" class="mw-redirect" title="Totalitarian regime">totalitarian regimes</a>, and, sitting between these two, <a href="/wiki/Authoritarianism" title="Authoritarianism">authoritarian regimes</a> with a variety of <a href="/wiki/Hybrid_regime" title="Hybrid regime">hybrid regimes</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Dobratz&#95;2015&#95;p.&#95;47&#95;1-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Dobratz_2015_p._47-1"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>1<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-LinzLinz2000&#95;2-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-LinzLinz2000-2"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>2<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> Modern classification systems also include <a href="/wiki/Monarchy" title="Monarchy">monarchies</a> as a standalone entity or as a hybrid system of the main three.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Garcia-AlexanderWooCarlson2017&#95;3-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Garcia-AlexanderWooCarlson2017-3"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>3<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-4"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>4<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> Historically prevalent forms of government include monarchy, <a href="/wiki/Aristocracy" title="Aristocracy">aristocracy</a>, <a href="/wiki/Timocracy" title="Timocracy">timocracy</a>, <a href="/wiki/Oligarchy" title="Oligarchy">oligarchy</a>, democracy, <a href="/wiki/Theocracy" title="Theocracy">theocracy</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Tyrant" title="Tyrant">tyranny</a>. These forms are not always mutually exclusive, and <a href="/wiki/Mixed_government" title="Mixed government">mixed governments</a> are common. The main aspect of any philosophy of government is how political power is obtained, with the two main forms being <a href="/wiki/Election" title="Election">electoral contest</a> and <a href="/wiki/Order_of_succession" title="Order of succession">hereditary succession</a>.
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Definitions_and_etymology">Definitions and etymology</h2></div>
<p>A government is the <a href="/wiki/System" title="System">system</a> to <a href="/wiki/Governance" title="Governance">govern</a> a <a href="/wiki/State_(polity)" title="State (polity)">state</a> or community. The <i><a href="/wiki/Cambridge_Advanced_Learner%27s_Dictionary" title="Cambridge Advanced Learner&#39;s Dictionary">Cambridge Dictionary</a></i> defines government as, "the system used for controlling a country, city, or group of people", or "an organization that officially manages and controls a country or region, creating laws, collecting taxes, providing public services".<sup id="cite&#95;ref-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-5"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>5<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> While all types of organizations have governance, the word <i>government</i> is often used more specifically to refer to the approximately 200 <a href="/wiki/List_of_sovereign_states" title="List of sovereign states">independent national governments</a> on Earth, as well as their subsidiary organizations, such as <a href="/wiki/State_government" title="State government">state and provincial governments</a> as well as <a href="/wiki/Local_government" title="Local government">local governments</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-FOOTNOTESmelserBaltes2001&#91;&#91;Category:Wikipedia&#95;articles&#95;needing&#95;page&#95;number&#95;citations&#95;from&#95;July&#95;2022&#93;&#93;&lt;sup&#95;class=&quot;noprint&#95;Inline-Template&#95;&quot;&#95;style=&quot;white-space:nowrap;&quot;&gt;&amp;#91;&lt;i&gt;&#91;&#91;Wikipedia:Citing&#95;sources&#124;&lt;span&#95;title=&quot;This&#95;citation&#95;requires&#95;a&#95;reference&#95;to&#95;the&#95;specific&#95;page&#95;or&#95;range&#95;of&#95;pages&#95;in&#95;which&#95;the&#95;material&#95;appears.&amp;#32;(July&#95;2022)&quot;&gt;page&amp;nbsp;needed&lt;/span&gt;&#93;&#93;&lt;/i&gt;&amp;#93;&lt;/sup&gt;&#95;6-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESmelserBaltes2001[[Category:Wikipedia_articles_needing_page_number_citations_from_July_2022]]&lt;sup_class=&quot;noprint_Inline-Template_&quot;_style=&quot;white-space:nowrap;&quot;&gt;&amp;#91;&lt;i&gt;[[Wikipedia:Citing_sources|&lt;span_title=&quot;This_citation_requires_a_reference_to_the_specific_page_or_range_of_pages_in_which_the_material_appears.&amp;#32;(July_2022)&quot;&gt;page&amp;nbsp;needed&lt;/span&gt;]]&lt;/i&gt;&amp;#93;&lt;/sup&gt;-6"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>6<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>The word <i>government</i> derives from the Greek verb <span title="Ancient Greek (to 1453)-language text"><span lang="grc">κυβερνάω</span></span> [<span title="Ancient Greek (to 1453)-language romanization"><i lang="grc-Latn">kubernáo</i></span>] meaning <i>to steer</i> with a <a href="/wiki/Gubernaculum_(classical)" title="Gubernaculum (classical)">gubernaculum</a> (rudder), the metaphorical sense being attested in the literature of <a href="/wiki/Classical_antiquity" title="Classical antiquity">classical antiquity</a>, including <a href="/wiki/Plato" title="Plato">Plato</a>'s <a href="/wiki/Ship_of_State" title="Ship of State">Ship of State</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-FOOTNOTEBrock201353–62&#95;7-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrock201353–62-7"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>7<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> In <a href="/wiki/British_English" title="British English">British English</a>, "government" sometimes refers to what's also known as a "<a href="/wiki/Ministry_(collective_executive)" title="Ministry (collective executive)">ministry</a>" or an "<a href="/wiki/Administration_(government)" title="Administration (government)">administration</a>", i.e., the policies and government officials of a particular executive or governing <a href="/wiki/Coalition_government" title="Coalition government">coalition</a>. Finally, <i>government</i> is also sometimes used in English as a <a href="/wiki/Synonym" title="Synonym">synonym</a> for rule or governance.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-8"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>8<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>In other languages, <a href="/wiki/Cognate" title="Cognate">cognates</a> may have a narrower scope, such as the <a href="/wiki/Government_of_Portugal" title="Government of Portugal">government of Portugal</a>, which is more similar to the concept of <a href="/wiki/Administration_(government)" title="Administration (government)">"administration"</a>.
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="History">History</h2></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1320445320" /><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Political_history_of_the_world" title="Political history of the world">Political history of the world</a> and <a href="/wiki/Political_philosophy" title="Political philosophy">Political philosophy</a></div>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Earliest_governments">Earliest governments</h3></div>
<p>The moment and place that the phenomenon of human government developed is lost in time; however, history does record the formations of early governments. About 5,000 years ago, the first small city-states appeared.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-FOOTNOTEChristian2004245&#95;9-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEChristian2004245-9"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>9<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> By the third to second millenniums BC, some of these had developed into larger governed areas: <a href="/wiki/Sumer" title="Sumer">Sumer</a>, <a href="/wiki/Ancient_Egypt" title="Ancient Egypt">ancient Egypt</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Indus_Valley_civilization" class="mw-redirect" title="Indus Valley civilization">Indus Valley civilization</a>, and the <a href="/wiki/List_of_Neolithic_cultures_of_China" title="List of Neolithic cultures of China">Yellow River civilization</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-FOOTNOTEChristian2004294&#95;10-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEChristian2004294-10"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>10<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>One reason that explains the emergence of governments includes agriculture. Since the <a href="/wiki/Neolithic_Revolution" title="Neolithic Revolution">Neolithic Revolution</a>, agriculture has been an efficient method to create food surplus. This enabled people to specialize in non-agricultural activities. Some of them included being able to rule over others as an external authority. Others included social experimentation with diverse governance models. Both these activities formed the basis of governments.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Eagly99&#95;11-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Eagly99-11"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>11<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> These governments gradually became more complex as agriculture supported larger and denser populations, creating new <a href="/wiki/Culture" title="Culture">interactions</a> and <a href="/wiki/Social_issue" title="Social issue">social pressures</a> that the government needed to control. <a href="/wiki/David_Christian_(historian)" title="David Christian (historian)">David Christian</a> explains
</p>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1244412712">.mw-parser-output .templatequote{overflow:hidden;margin:1em 0;padding:0 32px}.mw-parser-output .templatequotecite{line-height:1.5em;text-align:left;margin-top:0}@media(min-width:500px){.mw-parser-output .templatequotecite{padding-left:1.6em}}</style><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>As farming populations gathered in larger and denser communities, interactions between different groups increased and the social pressure rose until, in a striking parallel with star formation, new structures suddenly appeared, together with a new level of complexity. Like stars, cities and states reorganize and energize the smaller objects within their gravitational field.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-FOOTNOTEChristian2004245&#95;9-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEChristian2004245-9"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>9<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup></p></blockquote>
<p>Another explanation includes the need to properly manage infrastructure projects such as water infrastructure. Historically, this required centralized administration and complex social organisation, as seen in regions like Mesopotamia.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Fukuyama-2012&#95;12-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Fukuyama-2012-12"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>12<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> However, there is archaeological evidence that shows similar successes with more egalitarian and decentralized complex societies.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-13"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>13<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Modern_governments">Modern governments</h3></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:The_Harmsworth_atlas_and_Gazetter_1908_Government_systems_world_map.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ad/The_Harmsworth_atlas_and_Gazetter_1908_Government_systems_world_map.jpg/500px-The_Harmsworth_atlas_and_Gazetter_1908_Government_systems_world_map.jpg" decoding="async" width="350" height="199" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ad/The_Harmsworth_atlas_and_Gazetter_1908_Government_systems_world_map.jpg/960px-The_Harmsworth_atlas_and_Gazetter_1908_Government_systems_world_map.jpg 1.5x" data-file-width="2603" data-file-height="1481" /></a><figcaption>Forms of government in 1908 from <i>The Harmsworth atlas and Gazetter</i></figcaption></figure>
<p>Starting at the end of the 17th century, the prevalence of republican forms of government grew. The <a href="/wiki/English_Civil_War" title="English Civil War">English Civil War</a> and <a href="/wiki/Glorious_Revolution" title="Glorious Revolution">Glorious Revolution</a> in England, the <a href="/wiki/American_Revolution" title="American Revolution">American Revolution</a>, and the <a href="/wiki/French_Revolution" title="French Revolution">French Revolution</a> contributed to the growth of representative forms of government. The <a href="/wiki/Soviet_Union" title="Soviet Union">Soviet Union</a> was the first large country to have a <a href="/wiki/Communist" class="mw-redirect" title="Communist">Communist</a> government.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-FOOTNOTESmelserBaltes2001&#91;&#91;Category:Wikipedia&#95;articles&#95;needing&#95;page&#95;number&#95;citations&#95;from&#95;July&#95;2022&#93;&#93;&lt;sup&#95;class=&quot;noprint&#95;Inline-Template&#95;&quot;&#95;style=&quot;white-space:nowrap;&quot;&gt;&amp;#91;&lt;i&gt;&#91;&#91;Wikipedia:Citing&#95;sources&#124;&lt;span&#95;title=&quot;This&#95;citation&#95;requires&#95;a&#95;reference&#95;to&#95;the&#95;specific&#95;page&#95;or&#95;range&#95;of&#95;pages&#95;in&#95;which&#95;the&#95;material&#95;appears.&amp;#32;(July&#95;2022)&quot;&gt;page&amp;nbsp;needed&lt;/span&gt;&#93;&#93;&lt;/i&gt;&amp;#93;&lt;/sup&gt;&#95;6-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESmelserBaltes2001[[Category:Wikipedia_articles_needing_page_number_citations_from_July_2022]]&lt;sup_class=&quot;noprint_Inline-Template_&quot;_style=&quot;white-space:nowrap;&quot;&gt;&amp;#91;&lt;i&gt;[[Wikipedia:Citing_sources|&lt;span_title=&quot;This_citation_requires_a_reference_to_the_specific_page_or_range_of_pages_in_which_the_material_appears.&amp;#32;(July_2022)&quot;&gt;page&amp;nbsp;needed&lt;/span&gt;]]&lt;/i&gt;&amp;#93;&lt;/sup&gt;-6"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>6<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> Since the fall of the <a href="/wiki/Berlin_Wall" title="Berlin Wall">Berlin Wall</a>, <a href="/wiki/Liberal_democracy" title="Liberal democracy">liberal democracy</a> has become an even more prevalent form of government.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-FOOTNOTEKuperKuper2008&#91;&#91;Category:Wikipedia&#95;articles&#95;needing&#95;page&#95;number&#95;citations&#95;from&#95;July&#95;2022&#93;&#93;&lt;sup&#95;class=&quot;noprint&#95;Inline-Template&#95;&quot;&#95;style=&quot;white-space:nowrap;&quot;&gt;&amp;#91;&lt;i&gt;&#91;&#91;Wikipedia:Citing&#95;sources&#124;&lt;span&#95;title=&quot;This&#95;citation&#95;requires&#95;a&#95;reference&#95;to&#95;the&#95;specific&#95;page&#95;or&#95;range&#95;of&#95;pages&#95;in&#95;which&#95;the&#95;material&#95;appears.&amp;#32;(July&#95;2022)&quot;&gt;page&amp;nbsp;needed&lt;/span&gt;&#93;&#93;&lt;/i&gt;&amp;#93;&lt;/sup&gt;&#95;14-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKuperKuper2008[[Category:Wikipedia_articles_needing_page_number_citations_from_July_2022]]&lt;sup_class=&quot;noprint_Inline-Template_&quot;_style=&quot;white-space:nowrap;&quot;&gt;&amp;#91;&lt;i&gt;[[Wikipedia:Citing_sources|&lt;span_title=&quot;This_citation_requires_a_reference_to_the_specific_page_or_range_of_pages_in_which_the_material_appears.&amp;#32;(July_2022)&quot;&gt;page&amp;nbsp;needed&lt;/span&gt;]]&lt;/i&gt;&amp;#93;&lt;/sup&gt;-14"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>14<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>In the nineteenth and twentieth centuries, there was a significant increase in the size and scale of government at the national level.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-FOOTNOTEHaider-Markel2014&#91;&#91;Category:Wikipedia&#95;articles&#95;needing&#95;page&#95;number&#95;citations&#95;from&#95;July&#95;2022&#93;&#93;&lt;sup&#95;class=&quot;noprint&#95;Inline-Template&#95;&quot;&#95;style=&quot;white-space:nowrap;&quot;&gt;&amp;#91;&lt;i&gt;&#91;&#91;Wikipedia:Citing&#95;sources&#124;&lt;span&#95;title=&quot;This&#95;citation&#95;requires&#95;a&#95;reference&#95;to&#95;the&#95;specific&#95;page&#95;or&#95;range&#95;of&#95;pages&#95;in&#95;which&#95;the&#95;material&#95;appears.&amp;#32;(July&#95;2022)&quot;&gt;page&amp;nbsp;needed&lt;/span&gt;&#93;&#93;&lt;/i&gt;&amp;#93;&lt;/sup&gt;&#95;15-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHaider-Markel2014[[Category:Wikipedia_articles_needing_page_number_citations_from_July_2022]]&lt;sup_class=&quot;noprint_Inline-Template_&quot;_style=&quot;white-space:nowrap;&quot;&gt;&amp;#91;&lt;i&gt;[[Wikipedia:Citing_sources|&lt;span_title=&quot;This_citation_requires_a_reference_to_the_specific_page_or_range_of_pages_in_which_the_material_appears.&amp;#32;(July_2022)&quot;&gt;page&amp;nbsp;needed&lt;/span&gt;]]&lt;/i&gt;&amp;#93;&lt;/sup&gt;-15"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>15<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> This included the regulation of corporations and the development of the <a href="/wiki/Welfare_state" title="Welfare state">welfare state</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-FOOTNOTEKuperKuper2008&#91;&#91;Category:Wikipedia&#95;articles&#95;needing&#95;page&#95;number&#95;citations&#95;from&#95;July&#95;2022&#93;&#93;&lt;sup&#95;class=&quot;noprint&#95;Inline-Template&#95;&quot;&#95;style=&quot;white-space:nowrap;&quot;&gt;&amp;#91;&lt;i&gt;&#91;&#91;Wikipedia:Citing&#95;sources&#124;&lt;span&#95;title=&quot;This&#95;citation&#95;requires&#95;a&#95;reference&#95;to&#95;the&#95;specific&#95;page&#95;or&#95;range&#95;of&#95;pages&#95;in&#95;which&#95;the&#95;material&#95;appears.&amp;#32;(July&#95;2022)&quot;&gt;page&amp;nbsp;needed&lt;/span&gt;&#93;&#93;&lt;/i&gt;&amp;#93;&lt;/sup&gt;&#95;14-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKuperKuper2008[[Category:Wikipedia_articles_needing_page_number_citations_from_July_2022]]&lt;sup_class=&quot;noprint_Inline-Template_&quot;_style=&quot;white-space:nowrap;&quot;&gt;&amp;#91;&lt;i&gt;[[Wikipedia:Citing_sources|&lt;span_title=&quot;This_citation_requires_a_reference_to_the_specific_page_or_range_of_pages_in_which_the_material_appears.&amp;#32;(July_2022)&quot;&gt;page&amp;nbsp;needed&lt;/span&gt;]]&lt;/i&gt;&amp;#93;&lt;/sup&gt;-14"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>14<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Political_science">Political science</h2></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1320445320" /><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Political_science" title="Political science">Political science</a></div>

<span class="nowrap"><span class="mw-image-border noviewer" typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:A_coloured_voting_box.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="icon" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/0/01/A_coloured_voting_box.svg/20px-A_coloured_voting_box.svg.png" decoding="async" width="15" height="15" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/0/01/A_coloured_voting_box.svg/40px-A_coloured_voting_box.svg.png 1.5x" data-file-width="160" data-file-height="160" /></a></span> </span><a href="/wiki/Portal:Politics" title="Portal:Politics">Politics&#32;portal</a></td></tr><tr><td class="sidebar-navbar"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1333133064" /><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1239400231" /><div class="navbar plainlinks hlist navbar-mini"><ul><li class="nv-view"><a href="/wiki/Template:Politics_sidebar" title="Template:Politics sidebar"><abbr title="View this template">v</abbr></a></li><li class="nv-talk"><a href="/wiki/Template_talk:Politics_sidebar" title="Template talk:Politics sidebar"><abbr title="Discuss this template">t</abbr></a></li><li class="nv-edit"><a href="/wiki/Special:EditPage/Template:Politics_sidebar" title="Special:EditPage/Template:Politics sidebar"><abbr title="Edit this template">e</abbr></a></li></ul></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Classification">Classification</h3></div>
<p>In political science, it has long been a goal to create a typology or taxonomy of <a href="/wiki/Polities" class="mw-redirect" title="Polities">polities</a>, as typologies of political systems are not obvious.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-FOOTNOTELewellen2003&#91;&#91;Category:Wikipedia&#95;articles&#95;needing&#95;page&#95;number&#95;citations&#95;from&#95;July&#95;2022&#93;&#93;&lt;sup&#95;class=&quot;noprint&#95;Inline-Template&#95;&quot;&#95;style=&quot;white-space:nowrap;&quot;&gt;&amp;#91;&lt;i&gt;&#91;&#91;Wikipedia:Citing&#95;sources&#124;&lt;span&#95;title=&quot;This&#95;citation&#95;requires&#95;a&#95;reference&#95;to&#95;the&#95;specific&#95;page&#95;or&#95;range&#95;of&#95;pages&#95;in&#95;which&#95;the&#95;material&#95;appears.&amp;#32;(July&#95;2022)&quot;&gt;page&amp;nbsp;needed&lt;/span&gt;&#93;&#93;&lt;/i&gt;&amp;#93;&lt;/sup&gt;&#95;16-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELewellen2003[[Category:Wikipedia_articles_needing_page_number_citations_from_July_2022]]&lt;sup_class=&quot;noprint_Inline-Template_&quot;_style=&quot;white-space:nowrap;&quot;&gt;&amp;#91;&lt;i&gt;[[Wikipedia:Citing_sources|&lt;span_title=&quot;This_citation_requires_a_reference_to_the_specific_page_or_range_of_pages_in_which_the_material_appears.&amp;#32;(July_2022)&quot;&gt;page&amp;nbsp;needed&lt;/span&gt;]]&lt;/i&gt;&amp;#93;&lt;/sup&gt;-16"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>16<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> It is especially important in the <a href="/wiki/Political_science" title="Political science">political science</a> fields of <a href="/wiki/Comparative_politics" title="Comparative politics">comparative politics</a> and <a href="/wiki/International_relations" title="International relations">international relations</a>. Like all categories discerned within forms of government, the boundaries of government classifications are either fluid or ill-defined.
</p><p>Superficially, all governments have an official <i><a href="/wiki/De_jure" title="De jure">de jure</a></i> or ideal form. The United States is a federal constitutional republic, while the former <a href="/wiki/Soviet_Union" title="Soviet Union">Soviet Union</a> was a federal <a href="/wiki/Socialist_state" title="Socialist state">socialist republic</a>. However, self-identification is not objective, and as Kopstein and Lichbach argue, defining <a href="/wiki/Regime" title="Regime">regimes</a> can be tricky, especially <i><a href="/wiki/De_facto" title="De facto">de facto</a></i>, when both its government and its economy deviate in practice.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-FOOTNOTEKopsteinLichbach20054&#95;17-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKopsteinLichbach20054-17"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>17<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> For example, <a href="/wiki/Voltaire" title="Voltaire">Voltaire</a> argued that "the <a href="/wiki/Holy_Roman_Empire" title="Holy Roman Empire">Holy Roman Empire</a> is neither Holy, nor Roman, nor an Empire".<sup id="cite&#95;ref-FOOTNOTERenna2015&#95;18-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERenna2015-18"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>18<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> In practice, the Soviet Union was a centralized autocratic one-party state under <a href="/wiki/Joseph_Stalin" title="Joseph Stalin">Joseph Stalin</a>.
</p><p>Identifying a form of government can be challenging because many <a href="/wiki/Political_system" title="Political system">political systems</a> originate from socio-economic movements, and the parties that carry those movements into power often name themselves after those ideologies. These parties may have competing political ideologies and strong ties to particular forms of government. As a result, the movements themselves can sometimes be mistakenly considered as forms of government, rather than the ideologies that influence the governing system.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-19"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>19<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>Other complications include general non-consensus or deliberate "<a href="/wiki/Disinformation" title="Disinformation">distortion or bias</a>" of reasonable technical definitions of political ideologies and associated forms of governing, due to the nature of politics in the modern era. For example: The meaning of "conservatism" in the United States has little in common with the way the word's definition is used elsewhere. As Ribuffo notes, "what Americans now call conservatism much of the world calls liberalism or <a href="/wiki/Neoliberalism" title="Neoliberalism">neoliberalism</a>"; a "conservative" in Finland would be labelled a "<a href="/wiki/Socialist" class="mw-redirect" title="Socialist">socialist</a>" in the United States.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-FOOTNOTERibuffo20112–6quote&#95;on&#95;p.&#95;6&#95;20-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERibuffo20112–6quote_on_p._6-20"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>20<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> Since the 1950s, conservatism in the United States has been chiefly associated with <a href="/wiki/Right-wing_politics" title="Right-wing politics">right-wing politics</a> and the <a href="/wiki/History_of_the_Republican_Party_(United_States)" title="History of the Republican Party (United States)">Republican Party</a>. However, during the era of <a href="/wiki/Racial_segregation_in_the_United_States" title="Racial segregation in the United States">segregation</a> many <a href="/wiki/Southern_Democrats" title="Southern Democrats">Southern Democrats</a> were conservatives, and they played a key role in the <a href="/wiki/Conservative_coalition" title="Conservative coalition">conservative coalition</a> that controlled Congress from 1937 to 1963.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-FOOTNOTEFrederickson200012&#95;21-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFrederickson200012-21"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>21<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-22" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-22"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>a<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Social-political_ambiguity">Social-political ambiguity</h3></div>
<p>Opinions vary by individuals concerning the types and properties of governments that exist. "Shades of gray" are commonplace in any government and its corresponding classification. Even the most liberal democracies limit rival political activity to one extent or another while the most tyrannical dictatorships must organize a broad base of support thereby creating difficulties for "<a href="/wiki/Pigeonholing" title="Pigeonholing">pigeonholing</a>" governments into narrow categories. Examples include the claims of the <a href="/wiki/Plutocracy#United_States" title="Plutocracy">United States as being a plutocracy</a> rather than a democracy since some American voters believe elections are being manipulated by wealthy <a href="/wiki/Super_PACs" class="mw-redirect" title="Super PACs">Super PACs</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-FOOTNOTEFreeland2012&#95;23-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEFreeland2012-23"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>22<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> Some consider that government is to be reconceptualised where in times of climatic change the needs and desires of the individual are reshaped to generate sufficiency for all.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>23<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Measurement_of_governing">Measurement of governing</h2></div>
<p>The quality of a government can be measured by <a href="/wiki/Government_effectiveness_index" title="Government effectiveness index">Government effectiveness index</a>, which relates to <a href="/wiki/Political_efficacy" title="Political efficacy">political efficacy</a> and <a href="/wiki/State_capacity" title="State capacity">state capacity</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Guisan&#95;25-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Guisan-25"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>24<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Political_bias" title="Political bias">political bias</a> of a government can be measured relative to the <a href="/wiki/Median_voter_theorem" title="Median voter theorem">median voter</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-m117&#95;26-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-m117-26"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>25<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Forms">Forms</h2></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1320445320" /><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/List_of_forms_of_government" title="List of forms of government">List of forms of government</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1320445320" /><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Mixed_government" title="Mixed government">Mixed government</a></div>

<div class="sidebar-list mw-collapsible mw-collapsed"><div class="sidebar-list-title" style="color: var(--color-base)">Related</div><div class="sidebar-list-content mw-collapsible-content"><table class="sidebar-subgroup"><tbody><tr><td class="sidebar-content">

</tr><tr><td class="sidebar-below">
<span class="nowrap"><span class="mw-image-border noviewer" typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:A_coloured_voting_box.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="icon" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/0/01/A_coloured_voting_box.svg/20px-A_coloured_voting_box.svg.png" decoding="async" width="15" height="15" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/0/01/A_coloured_voting_box.svg/40px-A_coloured_voting_box.svg.png 1.5x" data-file-width="160" data-file-height="160" /></a></span> </span><a href="/wiki/Portal:Politics" title="Portal:Politics">Politics&#32;portal</a></td></tr><tr><td class="sidebar-navbar"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1333133064" /><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1239400231" /><div class="navbar plainlinks hlist navbar-mini"><ul><li class="nv-view"><a href="/wiki/Template:Basic_forms_of_government" title="Template:Basic forms of government"><abbr title="View this template">v</abbr></a></li><li class="nv-talk"><a href="/wiki/Template_talk:Basic_forms_of_government" title="Template talk:Basic forms of government"><abbr title="Discuss this template">t</abbr></a></li><li class="nv-edit"><a href="/wiki/Special:EditPage/Template:Basic_forms_of_government" title="Special:EditPage/Template:Basic forms of government"><abbr title="Edit this template">e</abbr></a></li></ul></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p><a href="/wiki/Plato" title="Plato">Plato</a> in his book <i><a href="/wiki/Republic_(Plato)" title="Republic (Plato)">The Republic</a></i> (375 BC) divided governments into five basic types (four being existing forms and one being Plato's ideal form, which exists "only in speech"):<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Abjorensen2019&#95;27-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Abjorensen2019-27"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>26<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Aristocracy" title="Aristocracy">Aristocracy</a> (rule by <a href="/wiki/Rule_of_law" title="Rule of law">law and order</a>, like ideal traditional "benevolent" kingdoms that are not tyrannical)</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Timocracy" title="Timocracy">Timocracy</a> (rule by honor and duty, like a "benevolent" military; Sparta as an example)</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Oligarchy" title="Oligarchy">Oligarchy</a> (rule by wealth and market-based-ethics, like a <a href="/wiki/Laissez-faire" title="Laissez-faire">laissez-faire</a> capitalist state)</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Democracy" title="Democracy">Democracy</a> (rule by pure <a href="/wiki/Liberty" title="Liberty">liberty</a> and <a href="/wiki/Social_equality" title="Social equality">equality</a>, like a <a href="/wiki/Freedom" title="Freedom">free</a> citizen)</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Tyrant" title="Tyrant">Tyranny</a> (<a href="/wiki/Rule_by_fear" class="mw-redirect" title="Rule by fear">rule by fear</a>, like a <a href="/wiki/Despotism" title="Despotism">despot</a>)</li></ul>
<p>These five regimes progressively degenerate starting with aristocracy at the top and tyranny at the bottom.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-FOOTNOTEBrill2016&#95;28-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrill2016-28"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>27<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>In his <i><a href="/wiki/Politics_(Aristotle)" title="Politics (Aristotle)">Politics</a></i>, Aristotle elaborates on Plato's five regimes discussing them in relation to the government of one, of the few, and of the many.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Jordović2019&#95;29-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Jordović2019-29"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>28<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> From this follows the classification of forms of government according to which people have the authority to rule: either one person (an <a href="/wiki/Autocracy" title="Autocracy">autocracy</a>, such as monarchy), a select group of people (an aristocracy), or the people as a whole (a democracy, such as a republic).
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Thomas_Hobbes" title="Thomas Hobbes">Thomas Hobbes</a> stated on their classification:
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1244412712" /><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>The difference of <a href="/wiki/Commonwealth" title="Commonwealth">Commonwealths</a> consisteth in the difference of the <a href="/wiki/Sovereign" title="Sovereign">sovereign</a>, or the person representative of all and every one of the multitude. And because the sovereignty is either in one man, or in an assembly of more than one; and into that assembly either every man hath right to enter, or not everyone, but certain men distinguished from the rest; it is manifest there can be but three kinds of Commonwealth. For the representative must need to be one man or more; and if more, then it is the assembly of all, or but of a part. When the representative is one man, then is the Commonwealth a monarchy; when an assembly of all that will come together, then it is a democracy or popular Commonwealth; when an assembly of a part only, then it is called an aristocracy. In other kinds of Commonwealth there can be none: for either one, or more, or all, must have the sovereign power (which I have shown to be indivisible) entire.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Leviathan&#95;30-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Leviathan-30"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>29<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup></p></blockquote>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Modern_basic_political_systems">Modern basic political systems</h3></div>
<p>According to <a href="/wiki/Yale" class="mw-redirect" title="Yale">Yale</a> professor <a href="/wiki/Juan_Jos%C3%A9_Linz" title="Juan José Linz">Juan José Linz</a>, there a three main types of <a href="/wiki/Political_system" title="Political system">political systems</a> today: <a href="/wiki/Democracy" title="Democracy">democracies</a>,
<a href="/wiki/Totalitarian_regime" class="mw-redirect" title="Totalitarian regime">totalitarian regimes</a> and, sitting between these two, <a href="/wiki/Authoritarianism" title="Authoritarianism">authoritarian regimes</a> with <a href="/wiki/Hybrid_regime" title="Hybrid regime">hybrid regimes</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-LinzLinz2000&#95;2-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-LinzLinz2000-2"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>2<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-Michie2014&#95;31-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Michie2014-31"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>30<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> Another modern classification system includes <a href="/wiki/Monarchies" class="mw-redirect" title="Monarchies">monarchies</a> as a standalone entity or as a hybrid system of the main three.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-Garcia-AlexanderWooCarlson2017&#95;3-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Garcia-AlexanderWooCarlson2017-3"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>3<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> Scholars generally refer to a <a href="/wiki/Dictatorship" title="Dictatorship">dictatorship</a> as either a form of authoritarianism or totalitarianism.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-ToddWaller2015&#95;32-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ToddWaller2015-32"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>31<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-LinzLinz2000&#95;2-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-LinzLinz2000-2"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>2<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-Sondrol&#95;33-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sondrol-33"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>32<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Autocracy">Autocracy</h3></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1320445320" /><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Autocracy" title="Autocracy">Autocracy</a></div>
<p>An autocracy is a system of government in which supreme <a href="/wiki/Power_(social_and_political)" title="Power (social and political)">power</a> is concentrated in the hands of one person, whose decisions are subject to neither external legal restraints nor regularized mechanisms of popular control (except perhaps for the implicit threat of a <a href="/wiki/Coup_d%27%C3%A9tat" title="Coup d&#39;état">coup d'état</a> or mass <a href="/wiki/Insurrection" class="mw-redirect" title="Insurrection">insurrection</a>).<sup id="cite&#95;ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>33<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Absolute_monarchy" title="Absolute monarchy">Absolute monarchy</a> is a historically prevalent form of autocracy, wherein a <a href="/wiki/Monarch" title="Monarch">monarch</a> governs as a singular <a href="/wiki/Sovereign" title="Sovereign">sovereign</a> with no limitation on <a href="/wiki/Royal_prerogative" title="Royal prerogative">royal prerogative</a>. Most absolute monarchies are <a href="/wiki/Hereditary_monarchy" title="Hereditary monarchy">hereditary</a>, however some, notably the <a href="/wiki/Holy_See" title="Holy See">Holy See</a>, are <a href="/wiki/Elective_monarchy" title="Elective monarchy">elected</a> by an <a href="/wiki/Electoral_college" title="Electoral college">electoral college</a> (such as the <a href="/wiki/College_of_cardinals" class="mw-redirect" title="College of cardinals">college of cardinals</a>, or <a href="/wiki/Prince-elector" title="Prince-elector">prince-electors</a>). Other forms of autocracy include <a href="/wiki/Tyranny" class="mw-redirect" title="Tyranny">tyranny</a>, <a href="/wiki/Despotism" title="Despotism">despotism</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Dictatorship" title="Dictatorship">dictatorship</a>.
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Aristocracy">Aristocracy</h3></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1320445320" /><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Aristocracy" title="Aristocracy">Aristocracy</a></div>
<p>Aristocracy<sup id="cite&#95;ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>b<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> is a form of government that places power in the hands of a small, <a href="/wiki/Elite" title="Elite">elite</a> <a href="/wiki/Ruling_class" title="Ruling class">ruling class</a>,<sup id="cite&#95;ref-OED&#95;36-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-OED-36"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>34<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> such as a hereditary <a href="/wiki/Nobility" title="Nobility">nobility</a> or <a href="/wiki/Social_privilege" title="Social privilege">privileged</a> <a href="/wiki/Caste" title="Caste">caste</a>. This class exercises <a href="/wiki/Minority_rule" class="mw-redirect" title="Minority rule">minority rule</a>, often as a <a href="/wiki/Land_tenure" title="Land tenure">landed</a> <a href="/wiki/Timocracy" title="Timocracy">timocracy</a>, wealthy <a href="/wiki/Plutocracy" title="Plutocracy">plutocracy</a>, or <a href="/wiki/Oligarchy" title="Oligarchy">oligarchy</a>.
</p><p>Many monarchies were aristocracies, although in modern constitutional monarchies, the monarch may have little effective power. The term <i>aristocracy</i> could also refer to the non-<a href="/wiki/Peasant" title="Peasant">peasant</a>, non-servant, and non-<a href="/wiki/Burgher_(social_class)" title="Burgher (social class)">city</a> classes in <a href="/wiki/Feudalism" title="Feudalism">feudalism</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-37" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-37"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>35<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Democracy">Democracy</h3></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1320445320" /><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Democracy" title="Democracy">Democracy</a> and <a href="/wiki/Types_of_democracy" title="Types of democracy">Types of democracy</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Democracy_claims.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c4/Democracy_claims.svg/500px-Democracy_claims.svg.png" decoding="async" width="380" height="193" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c4/Democracy_claims.svg/960px-Democracy_claims.svg.png 1.5x" data-file-width="940" data-file-height="477" /></a><figcaption><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1126788409" /><div class="plainlist"><ul><li><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959" /><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:green; color:white;-webkit-print-color-adjust: exact; print-color-adjust: exact;">&#160;</span>&#160;National governments which self-identify as democracies</div></li><li><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959" /><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:red; color:black;-webkit-print-color-adjust: exact; print-color-adjust: exact;">&#160;</span>&#160;National governments which do not self-identify as democracies</div></li></ul></div></figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Electoral_democracies.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/06/Electoral_democracies.svg/500px-Electoral_democracies.svg.png" decoding="async" width="380" height="193" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/06/Electoral_democracies.svg/960px-Electoral_democracies.svg.png 1.5x" data-file-width="2754" data-file-height="1398" /></a><figcaption>Governments recognised as "electoral democracies" as of 2022<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Government&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup> by the <i><a href="/wiki/Freedom_in_the_World" title="Freedom in the World">Freedom in the World</a></i> survey<sup id="cite&#95;ref-38" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-38"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>c<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup></figcaption></figure>
<p>Democracy is a system of government where <a href="/wiki/Citizen" class="mw-redirect" title="Citizen">citizens</a> exercise power by <a href="/wiki/Vote" class="mw-redirect" title="Vote">voting</a> and <a href="/wiki/Deliberative_democracy" title="Deliberative democracy">deliberation</a>. In a <a href="/wiki/Direct_democracy" title="Direct democracy">direct democracy</a>, the citizenry as a whole directly forms a <a href="/wiki/Participatory_democracy" title="Participatory democracy">participatory</a> governing body and vote directly on each issue. In <a href="/wiki/Indirect_democracy" class="mw-redirect" title="Indirect democracy">indirect democracy</a>, the citizenry governs indirectly through the selection of <a href="/wiki/Representative_democracy" title="Representative democracy">representatives</a> or <a href="/wiki/Delegated_voting" class="mw-redirect" title="Delegated voting">delegates</a> from among themselves, typically by <a href="/wiki/Election" title="Election">election</a> or, less commonly, by <a href="/wiki/Sortition" title="Sortition">sortition</a>. These select citizens then meet to form a governing body, such as a legislature or <a href="/wiki/Jury" title="Jury">jury</a>.
</p><p>Some governments combine both direct and indirect democratic governance, wherein the citizenry selects representatives to administer day-to-day governance, while also reserving the right to govern directly through <a href="/wiki/Popular_initiative" title="Popular initiative">popular initiatives</a>, <a href="/wiki/Referendum" title="Referendum">referendums</a> (plebiscites), and the <a href="/wiki/Recall_election" title="Recall election">right of recall</a>. In a <a href="/wiki/Constitutional_democracy" class="mw-redirect" title="Constitutional democracy">constitutional democracy</a> the powers of the majority are exercised within the framework of representative democracy, but the constitution limits <a href="/wiki/Majority_rule" title="Majority rule">majority rule</a>, usually through the provision by all of certain <a href="/wiki/Universal_right" class="mw-redirect" title="Universal right">universal rights</a>, such as <a href="/wiki/Freedom_of_speech" title="Freedom of speech">freedom of speech</a> or <a href="/wiki/Freedom_of_association" title="Freedom of association">freedom of association</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-39" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-39"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>36<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-britannica&#95;40-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-britannica-40"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>37<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading4"><h4 id="Republics">Republics</h4></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1320445320" /><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Republic" title="Republic">Republic</a></div>
<p>A republic is a form of government in which the country is considered a "public matter" (<a href="/wiki/Latin_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Latin language">Latin</a>: <i lang="la">res publica</i>), not the private concern or property of the rulers, and where offices of states are subsequently directly or indirectly elected or appointed rather than inherited. The people, or some significant portion of them, have supreme control over the government and where offices of state are elected or chosen by elected people.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-FOOTNOTEMontesquieu1748book&#95;2,&#95;chapters&#95;1&#95;41-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMontesquieu1748book_2,_chapters_1-41"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>38<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-Britannica&#95;42-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Britannica-42"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>39<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>A common simplified definition of a republic is a government where the head of state is not a monarch.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-WordNet&#95;43-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-WordNet-43"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>40<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-M-W&#95;44-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-M-W-44"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>41<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Montesquieu" title="Montesquieu">Montesquieu</a> included both <a href="/wiki/Democracy" title="Democracy">democracies</a>, where all the people have a share in rule, and <a href="/wiki/Aristocracy" title="Aristocracy">aristocracies</a> or <a href="/wiki/Oligarchy" title="Oligarchy">oligarchies</a>, where only some of the people rule, as republican forms of government.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-FOOTNOTEMontesquieu1748book&#95;2,&#95;chapters&#95;2–3&#95;45-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMontesquieu1748book_2,_chapters_2–3-45"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>42<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>Other terms used to describe different republics include <a href="/wiki/Democratic_republic" title="Democratic republic">democratic republic</a>, <a href="/wiki/Parliamentary_republic" title="Parliamentary republic">parliamentary republic</a>, <a href="/wiki/Semi-presidential_republic" class="mw-redirect" title="Semi-presidential republic">semi-presidential republic</a>, <a href="/wiki/Presidential_republic" class="mw-redirect" title="Presidential republic">presidential republic</a>, <a href="/wiki/Federal_republic" title="Federal republic">federal republic</a>, <a href="/wiki/People%27s_republic" title="People&#39;s republic">people's republic</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Islamic_republic" title="Islamic republic">Islamic republic</a>.
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading4"><h4 id="Federalism">Federalism</h4></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1320445320" /><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Federalism" title="Federalism">Federalism</a></div>
<p>Federalism is a political concept in which a <i>group</i> of members are bound together by <a href="/wiki/Covenant_(law)" title="Covenant (law)">covenant</a> with a governing <a href="/wiki/Federal_headship" title="Federal headship">representative head</a>. The term "federalism" is also used to describe a system of government in which <a href="/wiki/Sovereignty" title="Sovereignty">sovereignty</a> is constitutionally divided between a central governing authority and constituent political units, variously called states, provinces or otherwise. Federalism is a system based upon democratic principles and institutions in which the power to govern is shared between national and provincial/state governments, creating what is often called a <a href="/wiki/Federation" title="Federation">federation</a>.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-46" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-46"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>43<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> Proponents are often called <a href="/wiki/Federalist" title="Federalist">federalists</a>.
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Branches">Branches</h2></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Separation_of_powers.png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/29/Separation_of_powers.png/500px-Separation_of_powers.png" decoding="async" width="380" height="306" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/29/Separation_of_powers.png/960px-Separation_of_powers.png 1.5x" data-file-width="1327" data-file-height="1068" /></a><figcaption>Separation of powers in the <a href="/wiki/US_government" class="mw-redirect" title="US government">US government</a>, demonstrating the <i>trias politica</i> model</figcaption></figure>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1320445320" /><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Separation_of_powers" title="Separation of powers">Separation of powers</a> and <a href="/wiki/Fusion_of_powers" title="Fusion of powers">Fusion of powers</a></div>
<p>Governments are typically organised into distinct institutions constituting branches of government each with particular <a href="/wiki/Power_(social_and_political)" title="Power (social and political)">powers</a>, functions, duties, and responsibilities. The distribution of powers between these institutions differs between governments, as do the functions and number of branches. An independent, parallel distribution of powers between branches of government is the <a href="/wiki/Separation_of_powers" title="Separation of powers">separation of powers</a>. A shared, intersecting, or overlapping distribution of powers is the <a href="/wiki/Fusion_of_powers" title="Fusion of powers">fusion of powers</a>.
</p><p>Governments are often organised into three branches with separate powers: a legislature, an executive, and a judiciary; this is sometimes called the <span title="Latin-language text"><i lang="la">trias politica</i></span> model. However, in <a href="/wiki/Parliament" title="Parliament">parliamentary</a> and <a href="/wiki/Semi-presidential_system" title="Semi-presidential system">semi-presidential systems</a>, branches of government often intersect, having shared membership and overlapping functions. Many governments have fewer or additional branches, such as an independent <a href="/wiki/Electoral_commission" class="mw-redirect" title="Electoral commission">electoral commission</a> or <a href="/wiki/Supreme_audit_institution" title="Supreme audit institution">auditory</a> branch.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-FOOTNOTENeedler1991&#91;httpsbooksgooglecombooksidFcssj8yigS4CpgPA116&#95;116&#93;–118&#95;47-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTENeedler1991[httpsbooksgooglecombooksidFcssj8yigS4CpgPA116_116]–118-47"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>44<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Party_system">Party system</h2></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1333133064" /><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1246091330" /><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1288136931" /><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1246091330" /><link </tr><tr><td class="sidebar-below">
<span class="nowrap"><span class="mw-image-border noviewer" typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:A_coloured_voting_box.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="icon" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/0/01/A_coloured_voting_box.svg/20px-A_coloured_voting_box.svg.png" decoding="async" width="15" height="15" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/0/01/A_coloured_voting_box.svg/40px-A_coloured_voting_box.svg.png 1.5x" data-file-width="160" data-file-height="160" /></a></span> </span><a href="/wiki/Portal:Politics" title="Portal:Politics">Politics&#32;portal</a></td></tr><tr><td class="sidebar-navbar"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1333133064" /><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1239400231" /><div class="navbar plainlinks hlist navbar-mini"><ul><li class="nv-view"><a href="/wiki/Template:Party_politics" title="Template:Party politics"><abbr title="View this template">v</abbr></a></li><li class="nv-talk"><a href="/wiki/Template_talk:Party_politics" title="Template talk:Party politics"><abbr title="Discuss this template">t</abbr></a></li><li class="nv-edit"><a href="/wiki/Special:EditPage/Template:Party_politics" title="Special:EditPage/Template:Party politics"><abbr title="Edit this template">e</abbr></a></li></ul></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1320445320" /><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">"One-party government" redirects here. For a state in which a single political party controls the ruling system, see <a href="/wiki/One-party_state" title="One-party state">One-party state</a>.</div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1320445320" /><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Political_party" title="Political party">Political party</a> and <a href="/wiki/Party_system" title="Party system">Party system</a></div>
<p>Presently, most governments are administered by members of an explicitly constituted <a href="/wiki/Political_party" title="Political party">political party</a> which coordinates the activities of associated government <a href="/wiki/Official" title="Official">officials</a> and <a href="/wiki/Candidate" title="Candidate">candidates</a> for office. In a <a href="/wiki/Multiparty_system" class="mw-redirect" title="Multiparty system">multiparty system</a> of government, multiple political parties have the capacity to gain control of government offices, typically by competing in <a href="/wiki/Election" title="Election">elections</a>, although the <a href="/wiki/Effective_number_of_parties" title="Effective number of parties">effective number of parties</a> may be limited.
</p><p>A <a href="/wiki/Majority_government" title="Majority government">majority government</a> is a government by one or more <a href="/wiki/Governing_party" class="mw-redirect" title="Governing party">governing parties</a> together holding an absolute majority of seats in the parliament, in contrast to a <a href="/wiki/Minority_government" title="Minority government">minority government</a> in which they have only a plurality of seats and often depend on a <a href="/wiki/Confidence-and-supply" class="mw-redirect" title="Confidence-and-supply">confidence-and-supply</a> arrangement with other parties. A <a href="/wiki/Coalition_government" title="Coalition government">coalition government</a> is one in which multiple parties cooperate to form a government as part of a <a href="/wiki/Coalition_agreement" class="mw-redirect" title="Coalition agreement">coalition agreement</a>. In a single-party government, a single party forms a government without the support of a coalition, as is typically the case with majority governments,<sup id="cite&#95;ref-FOOTNOTEGallagherLaverMair2006&#95;48-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGallagherLaverMair2006-48"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>45<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-FOOTNOTEKettle2015&#95;49-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKettle2015-49"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>46<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> but even a minority government may consist of just one party unable to find a willing coalition partner at the moment.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-FOOTNOTEDuxbury2021&#95;50-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDuxbury2021-50"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>47<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>A state that continuously maintains a single-party government within a (nominally) multiparty system possesses a <a href="/wiki/Dominant-party_system" title="Dominant-party system">dominant-party system</a>. In a (nondemocratic) <a href="/wiki/One-party_system" class="mw-redirect" title="One-party system">one-party system</a> a single <a href="/wiki/Ruling_party" title="Ruling party">ruling party</a> has the (more-or-less) exclusive right to form the government, and the formation of other parties may be obstructed or illegal. In some cases, a government may have a <a href="/wiki/Nonpartisanism" class="mw-redirect" title="Nonpartisanism">non-partisan system</a>, as is the case with <a href="/wiki/Absolute_monarchy" title="Absolute monarchy">absolute monarchy</a> or <a href="/wiki/Non-partisan_democracy" title="Non-partisan democracy">non-partisan democracy</a>.
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Maps">Maps</h2></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1320445320" /><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_by_system_of_government" title="List of countries by system of government">List of countries by system of government</a></div>
<p>Democracy is the most popular form of government. More than half of the nations in the world are democracies—97 of 167, as of 2021.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-IDEA&#95;51-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-IDEA-51"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>48<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> However, the world is becoming more authoritarian with a quarter of the world's population under <a href="/wiki/Democratic_backsliding" title="Democratic backsliding">democratically backsliding</a> governments.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-IDEA&#95;51-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-IDEA-51"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>48<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
